**Step 1:** The witness’s testimony (Testimony 6) says the victim was stabbed in the back. The photo evidence (Evidence 6) shows the victim facing west (left) while the attacker (the prisoner) is positioned to the west of her, facing east toward her. In that arrangement the attacker is behind the victim, not in front of her.

**Step 2:** Proposition 3 states that because the victim was stabbed in the back, the attack must have come from the front – the attacker had to be positioned in front of the victim at the moment of the attack.

**Step 3:** Applying Proposition 3 to the scene depicted in Evidence 6 reveals the contradiction: the photo places the attacker behind the victim, whereas the testimony (and Proposition 3) require the attacker to be in front of the victim for a back‑stab.

**Step 4:** No other listed propositions are needed; the spatial reasoning hinges on the relative positions shown in the photo versus the logical requirement for a back‑stab.

**Answer (evidence vs. testimony):** {"evidence": 6, "testimony": 6}

- **Prop 2:** O'Conner is positioned between the stage and the maintenance area.  
- **Prop 5:** The school banner is situated between the stage and the art room.

**Step 3:**  
If Juniper was moving toward O'Conner (Prop 2), she would be traveling from behind the stage toward the maintenance‑area side of the stage. The banner with blood, however, lies on the opposite side of the stage (between stage and art room – Prop 5). Therefore Juniper could not simultaneously be heading toward the maintenance area **and** be at the banner location where the blood was found. This creates a spatial contradiction between the testimony and the evidence.
